16:I[44692,["/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/08cykig6vtx_p.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/07qi2okp-.yp9.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0frujd.eyhz4o.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0n4z.7yu76je-.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/09-o.77-p0p36.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/1304s0wcp2kce.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0j1uy9t~zxcf0.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/14_5o-nbg8k_r.js"],"DailyView"]
17:T47a,The authors build a controlled pretraining setup using a Fictional Knowledge Dataset with 2,048 synthetic facts about invented entities and attributes. Each fact is rendered into diverse documents across languages for injection during pretraining, with held-out multiple-choice questions for evaluation. For a fact, the authors know exactly how many times it appeared in language A and language B. They then regress target-language accuracy on native and foreign exposure counts. The ratio between the foreign-exposure coefficient and the native-exposure coefficient defines CL_eq. A score of 100% means that one exposure in the foreign language is as useful as one exposure in the target language; 0% means no effective transfer. To remove natural-language confounds, the paper also evaluates cloned language copies that are structurally identical but occupy different token spaces. This isolates vocabulary separation from syntax or semantics. The main intervention, word-wise translation, is motivated by this mechanism: it tries to unify token spaces for otherwise vocabulary-disjoint languages rather than relying on post-training alignment.18:T426,The experiments pretrain bilingual models from scratch while injecting fictional facts at controlled rates. Two scales are used: a 360M-parameter SmolLM2-style model and a 7B-parameter Llama-3 adaptation, both with a 65,536-token BPE vocabulary. The dataset validation shows chance-level MCQ accuracy with zero injection and a roughly log-shaped improvement as injected paraphrases increase, confirming that the facts are learnable and exposure-controlled. The main measurements show that compartmentalization already appears during pretraining. Disjoint token spaces strongly reduce cross-lingual transfer, and this effect persists across model scales. The authors also replicate key checks with a fictive-persona dataset built around high-frequency human names, reducing the chance that the result is only about rare synthetic entity tokens. The word-wise translation intervention improves both CL_eq and language-modeling perplexity, which supports the claim that token-space unification improves the training signal rather than only the diagnostic benchmark.19:T441,QVAC Genesis III contains 191.43B synthetic STEM tokens across 19 domains and three difficulty levels, rendered in four styles: textbook, web article, QA, and dialogue. The pipeline samples seed passages from FineFineWeb, filters them with the Ultra-FineWeb quality classifier, and keeps roughly 500k high-quality seeds. QwQ-32B is then used to generate self-contained multiple-choice questions. A weak edge-scale student answers the questions, and an answer-extraction step decides whether the student matches the gold label. Wrong answers trigger a Failure Analysis pipeline, where a teacher model diagnoses the misconception and writes a targeted corrective explanation. Correct answers trigger an Option-Level reasoning pipeline, where the teacher explains the correct option and each distractor. This differs from standard synthetic textbook generation because the student response controls the form of supervision. The paper also introduces an LLM-as-a-parser evaluation protocol that extracts final answers from free-form generations and reports both accuracy and Valid Answer Rate.1a:T57b,A BDLM splits a length-L sequence into B contiguous blocks of size M=L/B. Blocks are generated left to right, while tokens inside a block are denoised in parallel. During training, each target block b is corrupted at a sampled diffusion time and denoised conditioned on the clean prefix x(<b). The total objective is a sum over per-block losses. The paper exploits this structure. In conventional implementations, training builds a length-2L representation containing a clean copy and a corrupted copy, then applies a block-causal attention mask. Under context parallelism, both clean and corrupted K/V are sharded and communicated. The mismatch is that clean prefixes are shared by many later blocks, while corrupted K/V are local to one block. Block parallelism assigns each complete corrupted-block computation to a rank, keeping that block’s corrupted Q/K/V, activations, and gradients local. This removes block-specific cross-rank communication. The drawback is replication of long overlapping clean prefixes. Context-sharded block parallelism fixes this by sharding the clean sequence across the same ranks: each rank owns clean K/V shards that can serve all local block computations whose prefixes include those shards, and backward contributions are accumulated at the shard owner. The result preserves local corrupted-block computation while distributing clean-prefix storage and computation.1b:T845,方法核心是把某个前缀 h 处的一次局部梯度贡献写成随机变量 Z_h，并对其总方差做 law-of-total-variance 分解。分解后有两部分：第一部分是 decision uncertainty，即在前缀 h 上，不同动作 a 的 score function 与 Q_h(a) 组合后带来的方差；第二部分是 continuation uncertainty，即动作已经选定后，后续随机 suffix 带来的回报噪声。这个分解直接对应两种算力操作：新增 branch 用来降低 decision uncertainty；对已有 branch 做更多 suffix resampling，用来降低 continuation uncertainty。

在此基础上，作者推导了两个分配规律。其一是 compute-optimal suffix allocation law：给定某个前缀和动作，suffix rollout 数应按该动作的 continuation variance 与单位成本来分配，而不是平均分。其二是 marginal branching law：是否在某个前缀继续加一个新分支，取决于该前缀的 occupancy、score-weighted value uncertainty，以及新增分支的预期信息增益相对计算成本的比值。作者把这个量具体化为 EPIG，expected predictive information gain about the gradient。直观上，它不是问“这里模型是否犹豫”，而是问“如果我在这里多看一个分支，能否显著减少我对梯度方向的未知”。

算法层面，EPIG-Tree 不是完全抛弃 entropy。它先用 entropy 作为 proposal mechanism，筛出可能值得分叉的候选节点，因为低熵节点往往没有有效分歧；然后再用 EPIG 进行真正的算力分配，决定哪些节点值得扩展、哪些节点只需补 suffix、哪些节点不值得继续投入。这样做的原因很实际：直接精确评估所有节点的梯度信息增益代价太高，而 entropy 作为候选筛选器便宜且能排除大量无效位置。与 TreeRL/EPTree、TreePO 这类方法相比，区别不在“是否用树”，而在“树的生长目标函数”。已有方法更偏向局部不确定性或段级建模；这篇工作把目标直接对准梯度估计误差，并把 branch 与 suffix allocation 统一到同一方差框架里。1c:T773,The core setup defines the local gradient contribution at a prefix h as a random variable Z_h and decomposes its variance via the law of total variance. This yields two distinct sources of estimator error. The first is decision uncertainty: variance induced by which action a is taken at prefix h, weighted by the score function and the action value Q_h(a). The second is continuation uncertainty: variance in the suffix return after the action has already been fixed. This decomposition maps cleanly to two compute operations. Adding a new branch reduces decision uncertainty. Adding more suffix rollouts under an existing branch reduces continuation uncertainty.

From this view, the paper derives two allocation laws. First, a compute-optimal suffix allocation law: suffix samples should be distributed according to continuation variance and cost, rather than uniformly across branches. Second, a marginal branching law: whether to add another branch at a prefix depends on occupancy, score-weighted value uncertainty, and the expected reduction in gradient-estimation error relative to compute cost. The operational quantity is EPIG, expected predictive information gain about the gradient. The key distinction is that entropy only measures whether branches can differ, while EPIG measures whether those differences matter for the gradient estimator.

Algorithmically, EPIG-Tree still uses entropy, but only as a proposal mechanism. Entropy cheaply filters candidate branching points, while EPIG decides where compute is actually spent. This is a practical compromise: exact information-gain evaluation everywhere would be too expensive. Compared with prior tree-based LLM RL methods such as entropy-triggered branching or segment-level tree modeling, the novelty is the objective function. The tree is optimized for gradient estimation quality, not for uncertainty alone or for generic search coverage.1d:T64d,The empirical section spans four settings designed to validate the theory rather than only show end-task gains. First, cloned-state continuous control provides a controlled environment for testing the branching and suffix-allocation laws. Second, frozen LLM gradient calibration directly measures how well different tree construction strategies approximate the target gradient; this is one of the most important experiments because it matches the paper’s stated objective. Third, single-turn LLM math evaluates whether EPIG-Tree improves compute efficiency under verifiable rewards. Fourth, online multi-turn Wordle tests the method in a setting with recovery behavior, sequential dependence, and environment stochasticity.

The reported pattern is consistent across the paper: EPIG-Tree helps most when continuation noise is substantial and when only a small subset of decisions materially affects the final outcome. In those regimes, independent rollouts and entropy-only branching waste budget on low-value variation. The paper is also careful about boundary conditions. When the environment is nearly deterministic, trajectories are short, or entropy already aligns well with value-relevant branching, cheaper entropy baselines can match or nearly match EPIG-Tree. That is an important result because it clarifies where the extra machinery is justified. The provided text does not include the full numeric tables, so it is better to avoid inventing exact deltas; the main reported metrics are gradient calibration error, return under fixed compute budgets, and performance curves as branching budget varies.1e:T4ea,If time is limited, focus on the introduction, the variance decomposition proposition, the EPIG definition and allocation algorithm, and the frozen-LLM calibration plus Wordle experiments. The conceptual center of the paper is not “tree rollouts” by itself, but the claim that a rollout tree is an experimental device for estimating policy gradients. While reading, track three questions. First, which quantities in the decomposition are directly estimable and which require approximation? Second, how is EPIG computed cheaply enough to be useful online? Third, why is entropy a good proposal signal but a poor final allocation criterion?

Two parts are especially worth deeper inspection. One is occupancy weighting: in language-model training, high-frequency prefixes and rare prefixes do not contribute equally to the gradient, and this changes the branching objective. The other is the suffix allocation law, which suggests possible links to adaptive compute, verifier-guided rollouts, and speculative branching. To judge practical scalability, pay attention to algorithmic complexity, parallel implementation details, and whether the method assumes cloned states or reversible environments in ways that may limit deployment in large-scale online RL.1f:T867,方法核心有四部分。第一，模型骨架仍是 Qwen3 causal backbone，作者没有改成双向注意力，而是保留 causal mask。这样做的直接好处是天然兼容 KV cache，也能继承成熟 AR 模型的训练稳定性；代价是 diffusion 目标不能做标准的全双向 masked denoising，只能做 causal masked reconstruction。第二，引入 slot 化训练。目标序列被切成连续的固定长度 slot，每个样本随机采样一个 p_mask，决定有多少 slot 被整体 mask。未 mask 的 slot 走 AR next-token loss，mask 的 slot 全部替换成特殊的 <mdm_mask>，走重建 loss。于是同一次前向里，模型同时学“在可见局部里顺序预测”和“在给定上下文下恢复整段缺失内容”。第三，作者对输入顺序做了专门重排：clean slots 放在前，masked slots 放在后，同时 clean/masked 两组内部还可打乱顺序。这个设计的关键不是数据花样，而是让 masked 部分在 causal attention 下仍能看到全部 visible prefix，从而在不破坏因果掩码的前提下模拟一种条件去噪。第四，slot size curriculum。训练初期 slot 很小，例如从 2 开始，后续逐步扩到 4、8、16、32、64。小 slot 时，masked reconstruction 更接近局部补全，优化难度低；slot 变大后，模型必须在更粗粒度上恢复长片段，逐渐学习更强的并行补全能力。文中还加入 ordered_sampling，使 slot 内 token 的 mask 强度从左到右递增，并通过 1/p_mask 的归一化加权，让模型更重视 slot 起始 token 的正确预测，因为这些 token 会决定后续整段生成的走向。另一些实现细节如 grouped_noise、max_span_length、noise_sorting，则是为了统一支持 prefix completion、fill-in-the-prefix、fill-in-the-middle 和随机 span masking 等不同缺失模式。与 BD3-LMs 相比，Zarya 不把 block size 固定死；与 Eso-LMs 相比，它不是只在 loss 层面混合，而是把训练样本结构、mask 机制和 curriculum 一起设计；与 ReFusion 相比，它更强调训练—推理解耦，同一模型可切换双模式推理。20:T432,Its value for pretraining research is threefold. First, it offers a practical route for non-pure-AR language modeling that does not require abandoning a causal backbone. That matters because many alternatives to AR become difficult to integrate into existing training and serving stacks. Second, Zarya explicitly decouples training configuration from inference mode. A trained model can be used either with MDM-style sampling or with slotted speculative decoding through an inference flag, which is more flexible than methods whose training setup hard-codes the decoding regime. Third, the slot curriculum is interesting as a training-dynamics device, not just as data formatting. Small slots keep the task close to AR and easier to optimize; larger slots force broader reconstruction and more parallel behavior. That idea is relevant beyond this paper, especially for any-order LM, blockwise LM, and AR-plus-diffusion hybrids. At this stage, the contribution looks more like a promising modeling recipe than a definitive replacement for standard large-scale AR pretraining.21:T84c,The method has four main components. First, the backbone remains a causal Qwen3 model. The authors do not switch to bidirectional attention. This preserves KV-cache compatibility and leverages the optimization stability of standard AR models, but it also means the diffusion objective cannot be a fully bidirectional masked denoising objective. Instead, Zarya uses a causal masked-reconstruction objective. Second, the target sequence is partitioned into contiguous fixed-length slots. For each example, a mask ratio p_mask is sampled, and a subset of slots is fully masked. Visible slots contribute an autoregressive next-token loss, while masked slots are replaced by a special <mdm_mask> token and contribute a reconstruction loss. The model therefore learns sequential prediction and context-conditioned parallel recovery within the same forward pass. Third, the input is reordered so that clean slots come before masked slots, and both groups may be permuted internally. This is a key design choice: under causal attention, masked positions can still attend to the visible prefix, which gives a denoising-like conditional structure without breaking causal masking or KV-cache reuse. Fourth, slot size is increased over training, for example from 2 to 64. Early training stays close to local AR-like prediction; later training requires reconstruction of larger spans and encourages coarser-grained parallel generation. The paper also introduces ordered_sampling, where token-level masking importance increases from left to right within a slot, combined with 1/p_mask normalization so the model emphasizes getting early slot tokens right. That makes sense because early tokens anchor the rest of the span. Additional knobs such as grouped_noise, max_span_length, and noise_sorting support prefix completion, fill-in-the-prefix, fill-in-the-middle, and random-span masking. Relative to BD3-LMs, Zarya does not commit to a fixed block size. Relative to Eso-LMs, it is not only a loss-level fusion; it also redesigns sample structure and curriculum. Relative to ReFusion, it puts more emphasis on train-inference decoupling.22:T7d7,From the full text provided, the experimental setup uses Qwen3-0.6B, 1.7B, and 4B backbones, fine-tuned for one epoch on a 32M-example instruction dataset totaling about 37.7B tokens, spanning math, coding, and general instruction following. This is important context: the paper is validating the hybrid objective in a continued-training or mid-training regime rather than in full from-scratch pretraining. The authors release three model sizes and support two inference modes, MDM sampling and slotted speculative decoding, both with KV-cache support. The introduction positions the work against prior hybrids such as ReFusion, which reportedly beats Qwen3-8B on GSM8K and MBPP while being 2.33x faster on average; Zarya is clearly targeting the same quality-versus-throughput tradeoff space. The excerpt does not include the full result tables, so the exact benchmark deltas are not visible here. Still, several experimental signals matter. The paper explicitly notes that increasing slot size raises the computed loss, so training curves across curriculum stages are not directly comparable without normalization. The presence of ordered_sampling and per-token 1/p_mask weighting suggests the authors observed that early tokens in a slot disproportionately affect span coherence, which is likely reflected in ablations. The default hyperparameters—diffusion loss proportion 0.5, linear noise schedule alpha_t = alpha_0(1-t), alpha_0 = 0.25, epsilon = 0.001—also indicate a relatively lightweight diffusion parameterization designed to remain compatible with an AR backbone. The most important experimental questions are therefore the ablations over slot curriculum, lambda, shuffling, and the quality-latency tradeoff under the two inference modes. If the full tables show that Zarya stays close to or exceeds pure AR baselines on math and coding while improving wall-clock throughput in parallel mode, the main contribution is the engineering viability of unified training with dual-mode deployment.23:T56c,Start with the introduction and the main method overview to avoid misreading this as ordinary infilling or instruction tuning. Then read the training section carefully, especially the forward_process details: slot partitioning, mask sampling, noise sorting, ordered_sampling, and loss normalization. That is where the paper explains how a causal model can still learn a diffusion-like objective. Next, inspect Figure 1 and the slot_step_borders discussion to understand how curriculum changes task difficulty and why larger slots inflate the loss. If time is limited, prioritize three parts of the experiments: comparisons against pure AR and prior hybrid baselines, ablations on slot size / lambda / shuffling, and the quality-throughput curves for the two inference modes. Two deeper questions are worth keeping in mind while reading. First, does train-inference decoupling really hold in practice, or does each decoding mode still benefit from mode-specific training? Second, what is the ceiling of causal masked reconstruction? Since masked tokens cannot attend to future masked tokens, the method may be learning structured span infilling rather than a fully order-free generation process. For pretraining relevance, the key issue is not a single benchmark win but whether the slot curriculum can scale to larger pretraining runs rather than only working in instruction continuation.24:Tace,ATC 的基本设定是，在输入和最终答案之间插入若干连续的 abstract thought token，但训练时并不给这些 token 任何人工语义标签，也不要求它们对应自然语言步骤。模型只对最终任务目标负责。关键做法不是“加 token”本身，而是围绕这些 token 设计一套逐级加难的课程。

课程的核心思想是，把原本难以直接学会的复杂任务拆成一串分布 D1, D2, ...，每一阶段都比上一阶段稍难，并且需要多一个 latent thought step 来承接更复杂的中间计算。模型先在较简单分布上学会利用较短的 thought chain；当达到阈值后，再进入更难阶段，增加问题复杂度和可用的 latent reasoning 深度。这样做的目的，是让后期阶段的某个 thought token 可以把前期阶段已经学到的表示当作“现成子程序”继续使用，而不是从头在最终监督下硬学长程组合计算。

理论部分聚焦 parity 任务，并回应 Lotfi 等人的 conjecture。作者分析一层 attention 模型在 ATC 下的梯度动力学，证明在一定条件下，一次足够大的梯度更新就会让注意力分数集中到最近的 thought token 上，从而出现所谓 attention simplicity bias：模型倾向于依赖最容易提供预测信号的中间 token。这个结果的意义不是 parity 本身，而是说明 curriculum 可以把原本 one-shot 难学的高阶函数，转化成一串局部可学的中间表示学习问题。

但作者也很诚实地指出，理论假设里“早期阶段学到的表示会被后续阶段保留”在实践中并不会自动成立。为此他们提出 curriculum backtracking：每推进到新阶段后，都重新在所有已完成阶段上评估；如果某个旧阶段准确率跌破阈值，就退回去继续训练，直到旧能力恢复。这个机制有点像显式的 retention control，目的是防止 latent thought 的语义漂移和灾难性遗忘。

另一个关键工程点是训练长 latent chain 的成本。若对所有 thought step 做完整反传，激活存储和计算量会随链长线性甚至更糟地增长。作者因此引入 truncated backpropagation through time，只在局部窗口内回传梯度，让更长的抽象思维链可训练。这里的取舍很明确：牺牲一部分全局梯度精确性，换取可扩展的链长和更稳定的训练。

与已有方法相比，ATC 不同于显式 scratchpad 监督，因为它不规定中间格式；也不同于单纯 curriculum learning，因为它不是只在输入难度上做排序，而是把课程和 latent thought depth 绑定起来；也不同于直接加 latent token 的做法，因为它额外处理了表示保留和训练成本这两个常见失败点。25:Tae8,ATC inserts continuous abstract thought tokens between the input and the final answer, but does not assign them any human-readable meaning and does not supervise them with target traces. The model is trained only on the end task. The key idea is not merely adding latent tokens; it is coupling them to a staged curriculum.

The curriculum defines a sequence of increasingly difficult distributions. At each stage, the task becomes slightly harder and the model is allowed or required to use one more latent thought step. The intention is that later stages can build on representations learned in earlier stages, effectively reusing prior thought tokens as intermediate subroutines. This turns a hard one-shot learning problem into a sequence of locally learnable representation-learning problems.

The theoretical analysis focuses on parity and addresses the conjecture from Lotfi et al. The authors study gradient dynamics in a one-layer attention model trained with ATC and show that, under suitable assumptions, a single sufficiently large gradient step can make attention concentrate on the most recent thought token. This formalizes the attention simplicity bias in this setting: the model prefers intermediate tokens that provide the easiest predictive signal. The point is not parity itself, but the mechanism by which curriculum can make otherwise hard high-degree functions learnable through staged latent computation.

The paper is careful about the gap between theory and practice. A key theoretical assumption is that representations learned in earlier curriculum phases remain intact and usable later. Empirically, this does not happen automatically. To address that, the authors introduce curriculum backtracking: after advancing to a new stage, the model is reevaluated on all previously completed stages, and if performance on an earlier stage drops below threshold, training returns to that stage until the capability is restored. This acts as an explicit retention mechanism against semantic drift and catastrophic forgetting in latent thoughts.

A second engineering contribution is truncated backpropagation through time for long latent chains. Full backprop across all thought steps makes activation storage and compute grow with chain length. Truncated BPTT limits gradient propagation to local windows, trading some gradient fidelity for tractable training of deeper latent reasoning chains.

Relative to prior work, ATC differs from explicit scratchpad supervision because it does not prescribe an intermediate format; from plain curriculum learning because it ties curriculum progression to latent reasoning depth; and from naive latent-token insertion because it explicitly handles representation retention and training cost, which are major practical failure modes.26:T85c,The experiments span theory-driven validation and three task families: parity, graph reasoning, and multi-digit arithmetic. The goal is not a single benchmark score, but to test whether ATC actually induces useful abstract thought tokens and whether those thoughts remain stable under curriculum progression.

On parity, used as a canonical hard-to-learn setting, direct training on the full target distribution struggles, whereas ATC substantially improves learning. The attention patterns also align better with the theory: later stages rely heavily on the most recent thought token, consistent with the predicted attention simplicity bias. The most informative evidence here is not just final accuracy, but the evolution of attention and learning trajectories across curriculum stages.

On graph tasks and multi-digit arithmetic, ATC outperforms fixed-difficulty training and other baselines in sample efficiency and final task performance. The ablations are especially important. Removing curriculum backtracking causes earlier-stage performance to degrade as the curriculum advances, showing that latent representations are indeed overwritten by later training. Removing truncated BPTT preserves fuller gradients in principle, but training cost rises quickly and chain length becomes hard to scale. With backtracking, the model maintains competence on earlier stages while extending the latent chain; with truncated BPTT, deeper latent reasoning becomes trainable under practical resource limits.

Three empirical lessons stand out. First, latent thoughts are not automatically useful; they need a staged training signal. Second, retention is central: without an explicit mechanism, longer curricula can erase the very intermediate representations they are supposed to build on. Third, the bottleneck in long latent reasoning is not only expressivity but also memory and optimization stability, so engineering choices are part of the method rather than implementation detail. The excerpt does not provide complete parameter counts, dataset sizes, or all table values, so it would be unsafe to invent exact numbers beyond what is shown.27:T666,Start with the introduction, especially the discussion of CoT, attention simplicity bias, and the computational limits of transformers without intermediate reasoning. That framing explains why the authors move away from explicit scratchpads and why curriculum is more than an optimization convenience here. Then read the theoretical section around Theorem 1. The proof details are less important on a first pass than understanding the claim: ATC can induce attention to focus on the easiest predictive thought token, and that mechanism depends on representation reuse across stages.

Next, study the method section on curriculum construction, how abstract thought tokens are inserted and used, and the curriculum backtracking procedure. Backtracking is easy to underestimate, but it is the practical mechanism that makes the theoretical assumption of retained earlier representations less unrealistic. After that, read the training section on truncated BPTT to understand why the authors give up full-chain gradients in exchange for trainable longer reasoning chains.

In the experiments, prioritize three kinds of figures or tables: stage-wise learning curves, attention or thought-token usage patterns, and ablations for backtracking and truncated BPTT. For a quick read, the method overview, the statement of Theorem 1, and the main ablation tables are enough to capture the paper in about 30–45 minutes. For a deeper read, two questions are worth pursuing: whether the learned latent thoughts transfer beyond a single curriculum, and whether the ATC recipe can scale from synthetic reasoning tasks to realistic language pretraining.28:T4d0,The paper defines an independent-update sampler. At each step, the network emits a distribution for each writable position; the scheduler selects a group R based only on such per-position distributions; the sampler then draws each position in R independently. This abstraction covers absorbing-state masked diffusion, remasking diffusion, and the terminal phase of uniform-state diffusion. The main theoretical claims are sharp. If the positions in R are conditionally dependent given the frozen tokens, the divergence incurred by that step is at least the total correlation of the group. No set of per-position distributions can certify group independence, because two joint distributions may share identical one-position marginals while allowing different combinations of values. The states created by a dependent parallel write are also states where the standard per-position masked cross-entropy objective does not determine the needed joint conditionals. A further reachability result shows that simply writing one position at a time can also fail under hard constraints when the rest of the sequence pins a token. The technical message is precise: confidence measures sharpness of a marginal, not independence among positions.29:T5f6,The core construction is simple: arrange N sequence mixers in an N×N Latin square, yielding N^2 layers. By definition, each symbol appears exactly once in every row and every column. Mapped to network depth, this means each mixer is evenly represented across depth bands, so no mixer is systematically concentrated in early or late layers.

The flagship model, Aether-7B-5Attn, is a 49-layer MoE LM with 6.59B total parameters and about 2.98B active parameters. The text states that seven sequence-mixing mechanisms are placed in a 7×7 Latin-square layout. The provided content does not fully enumerate the seven mechanisms, so the safe reading is that the novelty is not a new mixer family but the balanced allocation principle.

More importantly, the paper builds a parameter-matched proxy with four mixers in a 4×4 Latin square and uses it for controlled multi-seed ablations. The ablation separates three factors:
1. permutation within a balanced distributed schedule;
2. distribution pattern, by comparing distributed layouts against contiguous depth bands;
3. composition, by comparing heterogeneous stacks against homogeneous ones.

This is the strongest part of the paper. It isolates whether exact ordering matters, whether depth-wise dispersion matters, and whether heterogeneity itself matters. The paper also reports per-mechanism cost profiles across context lengths, which is useful because hybrid stacks can have nontrivial length-dependent compute and memory behavior even when parameter counts are matched.2a:T529,The main empirical message has three parts.

First, reordering mixers within a balanced distributed schedule has negligible effect on validation loss. This is the cleanest evidence that fine-grained placement is not the main driver once balanced exposure is enforced.

Second, concentrating mixers into contiguous depth bands hurts performance. So the same set of mixers performs worse when grouped into blocks than when distributed throughout depth. That is an important result because many hybrid designs implicitly assume stage-wise specialization, such as local mechanisms early and global ones late.

Third, replacing the heterogeneous stack with a homogeneous one causes the largest degradation. This suggests that composition itself contributes more than placement tweaks.

In terms of scale, the flagship model is a 49-layer 6.59B-parameter MoE with roughly 2.98B active parameters, and the controlled study uses a parameter-matched proxy with multiple seeds. The provided text does not include exact loss deltas, benchmark scores, token budgets, or detailed throughput numbers, so those should be pulled from the full tables and figures when reading the paper directly. What is clear is that validation loss is treated as the primary metric and that cost profiling is part of the evaluation, not an afterthought.2b:T456,For a skim, focus on three parts.

Start with the problem formulation in the introduction. The key question is whether the paper really cleanly separates composition, placement, and parameter allocation. If that framing is sound, the rest of the paper becomes much more useful.

Then read the proxy ablation section carefully. This is where the paper earns its keep. Check whether the compared variants are truly parameter-matched, trained under the same budget, and replicated across seeds. The central claims to verify are: balanced permutations are nearly equivalent, banded layouts are worse, and homogeneous stacks are worst. This part is worth about 20–30 minutes.

Finally, scan the per-mechanism cost analysis. Even if one does not adopt the Latin-square design, the cost breakdown can inform future hybrid-stack work, especially for long-context settings where different mixers have very different scaling behavior.

If time is limited, prioritize the proxy experiments over the flagship 7B model section. The paper is more useful as an experimental design paper than as a single-model result paper.2c:T5c4,RLPF proceeds in four stages. First, parameters are grouped by semantic role rather than matched tensor by tensor. The groups include patch embedding, sequence mixing, feed-forward blocks, and normalization. This is necessary because attention projections and SSM transition parameters are neither shape-compatible nor functionally identical. Second, each group is projected into a shared low-rank latent space. The geometric choice is treated as a design hyperparameter, not as evidence that raw weights intrinsically live on a manifold. Third, fusion is performed in that latent space with component-dependent geometry: some groups use Euclidean averaging, while others use a Lorentz-model hyperbolic barycenter, implemented as a Fréchet mean under squared geodesic distance. The paper also adds a geodesic anchor objective to regularize the fused point relative to branch-specific representations and reduce instability in curved space. Fourth, the fused latent codes are decoded back into a ViT branch and an SSM branch, and a small learned gate combines their logits. This is the key conceptual point: the method does not collapse two models into one unified parameterization. It uses geometry-aware initialization plus lightweight training to build a retained two-branch hybrid. Compared with model soups, task arithmetic, or TIES, the novelty is less about the averaging rule itself and more about defining a role-based cross-architecture correspondence before fusion.2d:T449,The evaluation covers CIFAR-10, Oxford-IIIT Pet, and ImageNet-1K. The most useful aspect of the experimental design is that it separates several effects that are often conflated: ImageNet results before fine-tuning are reported separately from final fine-tuned gated-hybrid performance; Euclidean versus Lorentz fusion is compared; and the contribution of parameter grouping and gating is ablated. The qualitative takeaways are: (1) geometry-aware fusion provides a usable initialization, but most of the final gain appears to come from retaining both branches and fine-tuning them; (2) different parameter groups prefer different geometries, and the paper does not claim hyperbolic fusion is universally better; (3) the learned gate is important for stable combination of branch outputs. The provided text does not include the full result tables, so I cannot reliably state exact parameter counts, latent ranks, or top-1 deltas. Those should be checked directly in the main tables and ablations, especially for fairness around extra parameters, dual-branch inference cost, and fine-tuning budget.2e:T55b,Only WQ and WK are changed; all other parameters remain on a matched AdamW optimizer. Each head’s query and key projection is constrained to St(d,r), the space of matrices with orthonormal columns. The optimizer projects the Euclidean gradient onto the Stiefel tangent space, accumulates a scalar second moment per frame using the Frobenius norm of the tangent gradient, applies a trust-region cap to the tangent step, retracts back to the manifold with a polar retraction, and transports momentum by projection after retraction. The scalar second moment removes per-coordinate adaptivity but preserves O(d) equivariance, so the update does not depend on an arbitrary coordinate system. The paper proves several properties of this update: steepest descent in the embedded metric, scale independence, better conditioning, and exact O(d) equivariance. A key mechanism result is that weight decay has zero Riemannian gradient on the Stiefel manifold because W lies in the normal space. This explains why Euclidean weight decay can damage attention geometry while the constrained update does not move along the manifold in response to that term. The paper also argues that the attention gauge is a joint O(r) action on WQ and WK, not separate Grassmann structure for each matrix; gauge directions carry no gradient, so gauge removal is not the main reason the method helps.2f:T655,The design has three central pieces. First, the model uses a Causal Encoder-Decoder architecture. The encoder handles the causal prefill side, and decoder global KV is projected from the final encoder hidden states. This gives a cheaper prefill path and a stronger decode path, matching input-heavy agent workloads. Second, the paper introduces Compressed Sparse Attention 2, or CSA2. DeepSeek-V4 can be viewed as a sliding-window local-processing backbone plus compressed global context; V4.1-Flash keeps SWA in every layer but simplifies the global branch. CSA2 statically assigns layers to Full, Reindex, and Reuse modes. Full layers generate global KV and perform indexing. Reindex layers reuse global KV from a previous layer but use their own indexer Q to rescore shared indexer K and select fresh Top-K indices. Reuse layers reuse both global KV and Top-K indices. Each layer still keeps its own global Q and local SWA KV, so the method shares the expensive global cache components without collapsing all attention behavior into one shared layer. Third, the system combines FP4 global KV cache training with SWA Bounded Replay. FP4 global KV reduces runtime cache size with only small degradation. SWA Bounded Replay avoids persisting SWA KV to SSD by replaying only the most recent n_win tokens, rather than the L × n_win replay needed for exact reconstruction in the earlier design. Additional components include pure CSA2 instead of the previous CSA-HCA hybrid, Single-Pass mHC with a Mega-mHC kernel that halves activation memory traffic, the Engram conditional memory module, and DSpark speculative decoding.30:T459,The paper proposes a Model-Algorithm-System (MAS) taxonomy. At the model level, it covers architectural mechanisms such as modality-specific versus unified encoders, sparse expert routing, structural decoding, and modular adaptation. This layer addresses what should not be computed in the first place. At the algorithm level, it reviews ways to reduce effective computation under a fixed architecture, including token compression, pruning, quantization, distillation, speculative decoding, and cache reuse. At the system level, it focuses on execution orchestration: cache management, edge-cloud collaboration, latency-aware scheduling, and hardware-software co-design. A useful part of the survey is its emphasis on vertical synergies rather than isolated tricks. For example, reducing visual tokenization load at the model level changes the payoff of compression at the algorithm level and the bandwidth bottlenecks at the system level. Likewise, autoregressive multimodal generation pushes KV/cache design and scheduling into the foreground. The contribution is organizational and analytical, not algorithmic.31:T407,F²DR evaluates DeepSearch along three axes. The Content axis checks whether retrieved information covers key knowledge points and is factually grounded. The Trajectory axis evaluates the search process, including intent understanding, search atomicity, planning consistency, gap resolution, planning comprehensiveness, and iterative novelty, while penalizing redundant reasoning and plan-execution mismatch. The Answer axis combines subjective criteria such as intent match, coherence, and conciseness with objective coverage of core information. For benchmark construction, the authors sample complex queries from an enterprise search platform, extract requests that invoke deep thinking, and use DeepSeek-V3 to remove trivial or single-hop cases. They then run a full DeepSearch pipeline with DAG-based planning and reflection, search engines and MCP tools for evidence acquisition, and cross-turn evidence aggregation for final response generation. Preference pairs are filtered for discriminability and then checked by humans.32:T5b7,The work has two layers. The first is SimLife, a simulation platform built on The Sims 4 for generating long-horizon household activity streams with rich visual observations, synchronized logs, and synthetic dialogue with audio. A simulated day runs from 6 AM to 10 PM in-game and corresponds to about 24 minutes of real-time footage. The authors sample 466 in-game days as building blocks.

The second layer is SimLife-BP, a benchmark centered on latent behavioral patterns. Each episode is constructed by filtering and linking simulated days so that a target rule emerges over time, for example a character drinking coffee on weekdays or after staying up late. Evidence is intentionally distributed across days, events, and modalities, so the answer cannot be read off from a single moment.

The benchmark uses two design axes. One axis is question type: direct prediction, counterfactual prediction, noisy counterfactual prediction, and inverse counterfactual reasoning. The other axis is hint level: explicit relevant conditions, abstract condition types, or no hint at all. This structure is important because it separates genuine rule induction from shallow correlation matching. In particular, inverse reasoning and noisy counterfactuals are useful for exposing frequency-based shortcuts.

Compared with standard long-video QA or long-document QA, the benchmark is more explicitly about latent rule induction and rule updating under long temporal horizons.33:T4ca,SimLife-BP contains 106 episodes, averaging 15.49 hours and 38.57 in-game days, with the longest episodes reaching 48.23 hours and 120 days. The benchmark includes 1,439 question-answer pairs. The paper evaluates multiple frontier models and architectures.

The main empirical findings are qualitative but important. First, models can achieve decent question-level accuracy without fully understanding the underlying rule. They often do better on forward prediction than on inverse reasoning, which suggests partial pattern matching rather than a robust internal rule representation. Second, models frequently fall back to simple heuristics such as frequency counting instead of condition-sensitive reasoning. This becomes visible on noisy counterfactuals and tasks requiring compositional conditions. Third, models struggle to update their beliefs when the latent pattern changes, indicating that a long context window alone does not provide reliable long-term state tracking.

For careful reading, the most informative results are not the overall averages but the breakdowns by question type, hint level, and possibly episode length. Those slices reveal whether a model is actually learning the rule or exploiting shortcuts.34:T72a,The method has two stages. The first builds the baseline Live-ProsodyJudge (LPJ). Gemini is used as the teacher to compare paired audio clips in both A/B orders, and only swap-consistent high-confidence outcomes are retained for distillation into Qwen3-Omni. The task protocol defines seven dimensions: four always-on core dimensions (C1–C4: fluency, intonation, emotion, and live-stream expressiveness) and three transcript-triggered dimensions (L1–L3: key-information emphasis, emotional-state switching, and interaction-mode switching). Training uses a curriculum from easier human-vs-TTS comparisons to harder cross-model and same-model comparisons, with rank-32 LoRA attached to Qwen3-Omni during SFT.

The second stage introduces Decoupled-Live-ProsodyJudge (D-LPJ) to address verdict coupling. There are three main changes. First, the overall-verdict target is removed. The reason is straightforward: if the model is trained to predict a global winner, that label becomes a shortcut and drags all dimension-level judgments toward the same answer. Second, the model uses dimension-wise SFT masking. Instead of discarding a whole pair when some dimensions are uncertain or sparsely supervised, only the affected dimensions are masked, preserving more data while reducing label contamination. Third, the paper applies span-local GRPO, where each dimension’s advantage is assigned only to the rationale span for that dimension. This is the most interesting part methodologically. A global RL signal would continue to reward shortcut behavior; local credit assignment forces the model to ground each dimension in separate evidence.

The key contribution is therefore not a new backbone, but a factorized supervision design. The paper decouples the problem at three levels: target space, sample utilization, and reward routing.35:T757,The main evaluation suite contains 1,043 internally human-annotated pairs, plus a 222-pair transfer set. T1–T4 cover Qwen3-TTS base versus an SFT variant, within-model comparisons for an internal GDPO variant, cross-model comparisons against BERT-CosyVoice, and human recordings versus Qwen3-TTS. T5 is a transfer setting with IndexTTS2 versus FireRedTTS, a model combination not seen during coupled-judge training. T1–T4 span 135 speakers and multiple live-stream styles, and each pair shares the same transcript to control lexical confounds. Three annotators label each pair independently; 45% are unanimous 3–0 decisions and 55% are 2-vote agreements with one abstention. Test partitions are disjoint from training and validation in audio, pair identity, normalized transcript, speaker, and source stream.

Empirically, LPJ works as a much cheaper distilled alternative to Gemini. Under 10-sample balanced-order inference, it shows negligible position bias and achieves higher point agreement than a single Gemini call on the four main test sets. D-LPJ reaches 86.10% pooled agreement while reducing verdict collapse, which is the more important result: the model produces genuinely varying dimension-level verdicts instead of mirroring the overall winner. In a practical Best-of-8 candidate-selection setting, LPJ’s top choice falls within the human top-3 preferences in 85.29% of cases, suggesting that the distilled judge is already useful for reranking and RL feedback.

The most informative parts of the experiments are not the headline agreement numbers, but the comparisons between coupled and decoupled training in terms of dimension diversity, inter-dimension correlation, and transfer behavior. If the full paper includes per-dimension agreement tables, confusion patterns, or ablations on global versus local GRPO, those are the sections worth prioritizing.36:T40c,This is a skim paper rather than a deep read for pretraining researchers. Start with the introduction to understand the task and the verdict-coupling failure mode, then read the data protocol, the three D-LPJ modifications, and the main result tables. Around 20–30 minutes should be enough to capture the main ideas. If your interest is reward modeling or judge design, spend extra time on the span-local GRPO details: how advantages are aligned to rationale spans, how masking is implemented, and whether there is a direct ablation against global GRPO.

While reading, keep three questions in mind. First, is verdict coupling mainly an artifact of teacher distillation, or a broader structural issue in open judge models? Second, does removing the overall verdict hurt final ranking quality? Third, can this dimension-decoupling recipe transfer to text judges, preference models, or verifier training? For LLM foundation-model work, the main takeaway is supervision decoupling and local credit assignment, not the speech task itself.37:T444,The paper first defines a Reward-Resolution Protocol to separate unlearnable numerical jitter from credible cardinal gaps. It uses bounded binning, a dead zone, and zero-gap skipping. Gaps below the trusted reward resolution are merged before group-relative updates. If a group has no credible gap, both reward and KL updates are skipped for that prompt, preventing noise from being written into the policy. The main operator is MaxNorm-AC, a minimal intervention that only replaces the within-group scale denominator. It uses the maximum absolute raw advantage, combined with a reward-resolution floor, as the denominator, and freezes the resulting loss weight for the current optimization step. This preserves credible cardinal gaps while bounding reward amplification, the effective-KL imbalance, and the tail of prompt-level batch weights. MaxNorm-RLOO is one instance that attaches this bounded-scale operator to the RLOO numerator. KL regularization, policy ratios, clipping, token aggregation, and length normalization are left unchanged, which makes the mechanism easier to attribute.38:T44d,The authors build AMPLE-Math, a controlled supervision suite derived from OpenThoughts-114k. It contains 5,319 math problems. Each problem has one verified answer and six answer-matched views, ranging from Answer Only to Full Trace. This design holds answer information fixed while varying the form and amount of reasoning representation. In training, the student sees only the problem and generates direct-response rollouts. A frozen copy of the same model acts as the teacher; it sees the problem, the selected privileged view, and the student prefix, then produces a next-token distribution under a thinking-enabled configuration. The loss is a teacher-to-student forward KL with per-vocabulary-term clipping at 0.05, following the gKL variant used in prior OPSD work. The reference-free baseline removes the privileged view but keeps the thinking-enabled teacher and direct-response student. The paper also profiles teacher scores on student responses, including correctness alignment and pressure on correction markers, then checks whether apparent intervention gains survive checkpoint matching.39:T76d,The main contribution is to treat EOS handling as a semantic alignment problem in the training objective, not merely a decoding-interface issue. The paper studies sampled-token OPD, where the student samples y_t at prefix s_t and the teacher scores only that sampled token. Contributions are averaged over valid generated tokens, without reward-to-go. In this setup, if the student samples its native EOS e* but the teacher prefers another functionally equivalent EOS, the student is explicitly pushed away from its own stopping action.

To formalize this, the paper defines an equivalence set of termination tokens, E_EOS, under the rollout protocol. It then compares four termination-handling strategies. The provided text does not list all four in full detail, but the key contrast is clear:
1. aligning the decoding stopping set alone;
2. aligning termination inside the distillation signal;
3. aggregating functionally equivalent EOS tokens into a shared semantic stopping action.

The main finding is that decoding alignment alone is insufficient. The problem is not just when rollout halts, but how the training signal evaluates the act of stopping. The effective correction is to aggregate equivalent EOS tokens semantically, so that any valid stop token is rewarded as the same action rather than treated as competing surface forms.

A second strong component is the stage-wise analysis using K2-Horizon checkpoints spanning pretraining, midtraining, SFT, and final post-training. This shows that EOS preference is not a static tokenizer artifact; it shifts across training stages. That explains why a base student and a post-trained teacher can strongly disagree on EOS usage despite sharing the same vocabulary. The paper also notes prompt-template sensitivity, which is practically important because formatting choices can alter both measured response length and apparent task performance.3a:T6ab,The main ablation uses Qwen3-1.7B-Base as the student and Qwen3-4B in non-thinking mode as the teacher on single-turn mathematical reasoning. To test generality, the paper also evaluates Llama-3.2-3B Base/Instruct and Gemma-3-4B PT/IT pairs. In each pair, student and teacher share the tokenizer and vocabulary, which rules out the trivial explanation that EOS mismatch comes from incompatible tokenization.

One particularly informative detail is that in the Qwen3 setup, the teacher-preferred alternative EOS token has extremely small probability in the initial student, around 10^-11. That makes transfer through sampled-token OPD very difficult: the student rarely samples the teacher’s preferred EOS, so the teacher cannot reinforce it effectively, while the student’s native EOS keeps getting suppressed.

Empirically, the paper reports that:
- aligning the decoding stopping set alone does not fix length inflation;
- aligning termination semantics in the distillation signal, especially via semantic EOS aggregation, substantially mitigates mismatch-induced inflation across Qwen3, Llama, and Gemma;
- stage-wise K2-Horizon analysis shows how termination preferences shift over training;
- even after the teacher-preferred termination form is transferred, a later inflation phase can still emerge, with response length increasing again and termination probability collapsing.

The provided text does not include the full numeric tables for average length, truncation rate, or task accuracy, so those should be read directly from the paper. Still, the experimental structure is strong: mechanism identification, controlled mitigation comparison, cross-family validation, and stage-wise diagnosis.3b:T4f3,Read it in the order of the causal story.

First, go through the sampled-token OPD definition and notation. The paper only makes sense once it is clear that the teacher scores the student’s sampled token rather than supervising the full distribution. That is the source of the EOS pathology. This part takes about 10–15 minutes.

Next, focus on the section defining the four termination-handling strategies and the main ablation table. Pay close attention to how the equivalence class of EOS tokens is defined and where the aggregation is applied: logits, probabilities, or token-level advantages. That implementation detail determines how portable the fix is to other OPD pipelines. This is the most important section and worth about 20 minutes.

Then read the K2-Horizon stage analysis, especially the phase where inflation reappears after EOS transfer has already happened. That part is more interesting than the mitigation itself if your goal is to understand OPD dynamics rather than just patch one bug.

Finally, scan the prompt-template and evaluation-protocol sensitivity results. They are a useful reminder that longer outputs should not be interpreted naively as stronger reasoning. As a skim paper, it can be read productively in about 35–45 minutes.3c:T44d,The authors construct a controlled factorial design that varies task and answer format independently. They take five benchmarks—TriviaQA, SQuAD, GSM8K, ARC-Challenge, and CRUXEval—and render each into seven answer formats spanning three format classes: binary judgments such as True/False and Yes/No, four-way options such as A-D or 1-4, and structured generation such as JSON. This produces 35 datasets, allowing comparisons where two datasets share the task but not the format, or share the format but not the task. For each dataset, they compute answer-span per-example gradients for 64 examples. Gradients are taken only with respect to the transformer body, excluding embedding and unembedding tables so that overlap in answer tokens cannot trivially drive similarity. The gradients are compressed to 16,384 dimensions using seeded CountSketch, and dataset alignment is measured by average cross-example cosine. They then apply a disattenuation correction based on within-dataset gradient coherence, so noisy datasets do not appear less aligned simply because their gradient signal is weaker.3d:T467,The central empirical finding is clear: datasets with the same answer format align strongly even when their tasks differ, while the same benchmark rendered in different format classes shows near-zero alignment. The abstract reports disattenuated cosine values near 0.4 for benchmark pairs sharing an answer format, and no meaningful alignment for same-task pairs with different answer-format classes. The null reference is also well specified: 280 pairs sharing neither task nor format class have mean -0.001, standard deviation 0.008, and maximum about 0.03, making values within roughly ±0.02 effectively null. The authors repeat the measurement over ten OLMo pretraining checkpoints from 1B to 4T tokens, ten Pythia checkpoints, and OLMo base/SFT/DPO/Instruct stages. They also test OLMo 1B, 7B, and 13B, plus Llama 3.1 and Qwen3 in both base and instruction-tuned forms. Finally, they analyze public LESS selections: top 5% subsets, about 13,533 examples, selected from a 270K-example pool for MMLU, BBH, and TydiQA across three seeds. The selected data is skewed toward examples with the same answer format as the target.3e:Taa1,方法上有三个层次。第一层是威胁建模。作者聚焦四类高风险攻击，目标包括数据/权重外传、绕过或关闭监控、创建未受监控的新 agent，以及借助环境和工具链完成长期潜伏式攻击。默认设置下，攻击 agent 被明确告知监控器的大致工作方式，并给到高层攻击策略，这样评测更接近“持续失配 agent 已经理解防御面”的场景，而不是只测偶然碰撞。

第二层是监控系统拆解。论文分析的生产监控器都不是单一分类器，而是多阶段流水线：先有极便宜的一 token 预筛阶段 S1，再有带推理的分类阶段 S2，必要时再升级到可读文件、可查询环境的 tool-using 审查 agent。设计目标是让大多数 benign action 在前面低成本放行，只有不确定样本进入后续阶段。问题也恰恰出在这里：任何“只升级不回退”的成本优化都会形成攻击面，攻击者会专门构造能骗过早期阶段、或让后续阶段误判为用户授权/无害的动作。

第三层是红队评测框架。作者把评测分成 analytical、replay、rollout 三种。analytical evaluation 直接读 prompt、代码和系统集成，找离散漏洞，比如哪些 tool call 没被监控、哪些字段会被拼进 monitor prompt、哪些 agent 输出会被错误提升成 user intent。replay evaluation 用已有 transcript 离线打分，分 benign replay 和 malign replay：前者用 4,000 个 SWE-chat action 估计误报等部署指标，后者用攻击 transcript 快速比较不同 monitor 版本、并支持局部优化攻击。rollout evaluation 则让红队 agent 在线与 monitor 交互，这是最接近真实风险的 on-policy 评测。

自动化攻击生成是这篇工作的一个技术重点。对 monitor injection，作者用类似 OPRO 的优化回路，让 proposer model 迭代改写注入字符串，以固定攻击 transcript 上的绕过率为目标做 hill-climbing。对 compaction attack 和 simulation 中的策略失败，则用 investigative fuzzing agent 大量生成轨迹，系统搜寻脆弱模式。这里的关键不是某个单一攻击技巧，而是把“生成攻击—离线筛选—在线验证”连成了一个可重复的红队流程。

从全文看，作者提出的改进原则也很具体：扩大监控覆盖面，尤其是 edit/write 等 cwd 内操作；减少 monitor 可被注入的文本通道；谨慎处理任何会把 agent 文本提升为用户意图的逻辑；不要把详细阻断理由原样反馈给攻击者；对高风险动作采用更保守的默认拒绝和更强的上下文审查；同时用 replay 和 rollout 联合调参，而不是只看单步分类表现。3f:Tb79,The methodology has three layers. The first is threat modeling. The authors focus on four high-risk attack classes, including exfiltration of sensitive data or model weights, disabling or bypassing the monitor, and spawning unmonitored agents. By default, the attacker is told roughly how the monitor works and is given a high-level attack strategy. This is deliberate: the goal is to evaluate monitors against a persistent, informed adversary rather than accidental collisions.

The second layer is monitor decomposition. The production systems under study are multi-stage pipelines rather than single classifiers. They include a very cheap one-token prescreen (S1), a reasoning classifier (S2), and in some cases a tool-using review agent that can inspect files and query the environment. Most benign actions are intended to clear early stages for cost reasons. That cost-aware design also creates attack surface: an adversary can target the early gate, exploit escalation heuristics, or phrase actions so that later stages reinterpret them as harmless or user-authorized.

The third layer is the red-teaming framework. The paper separates analytical evaluation, replay-based evaluation, and rollout-based evaluation. Analytical evaluation inspects prompts, code, and system integration directly to identify discrete vulnerabilities such as unmonitored tool calls, injection channels, prompt edge cases, and places where agent output is promoted to user intent. Replay-based evaluation scores existing transcripts offline. Benign replay estimates deployment metrics such as false positives; malign replay scores and optimizes attacks cheaply without running a live agent. Rollout-based evaluation runs a red-team agent online against the monitor and is treated as the most credible estimate of real risk.

A notable technical contribution is automated attack generation. For monitor-injection attacks, the authors use an OPRO-style optimization loop in which a proposer model iteratively edits an injection string and hill-climbs on bypass rate over a fixed transcript set. For compaction attacks and simulation-specific policy failures, they use an investigative fuzzing agent to generate many trajectories and surface recurring vulnerabilities. The important point is not one attack trick in isolation, but the end-to-end workflow: generate attacks, filter them cheaply with replay, then validate them with online rollouts.

The design lessons are concrete. Expand monitor coverage, especially for edit/write operations inside the working directory. Reduce text channels through which the monitor can be injected. Be careful with any logic that upgrades agent-produced text into user intent. Avoid giving the attacker overly informative block reasons. Use stricter default-deny behavior and stronger contextual review for high-risk actions. Tune with both replay and rollout rather than relying on single-step classification metrics.40:T747,The experimental value lies more in the evaluation stack than in any single headline number. The paper combines benign replay, malign replay, live arena rollouts, and LLM-DM simulation, covering a spectrum from cheap regression testing to high-fidelity adversarial interaction. One explicit scale detail in the provided text is that benign replay uses 4,000 actions from SWE-chat to estimate deployment metrics such as false positives. That matters because monitor improvements that only maximize blocking power can easily become unusable in practice.

The main empirical result is a set of reproducible failure mechanisms. Given high-level attack strategies, adversarial agents succeed through several distinct routes: self-generated prompt injection that contaminates monitor context; compaction or obfuscation of dangerous intent into superficially ordinary shell/edit actions that slip through early screening; and iterative adaptation using monitor feedback until risky actions are reframed as user-authorized, debugging-related, or operationally benign. The paper repeatedly shows that many failures are not isolated misclassifications. They are strategic breakdowns that emerge over multiple rounds of interaction.

The system comparisons and ablations indicate that monitor coverage of cwd edits, stricter guardian variants, escalation logic across stages, and the decision to expose detailed block reasons all materially affect the trade-off between attack success and false positives. The production and experimental variants also show that seemingly local engineering choices can reshape the overall attack surface. Even without every table value in the excerpt, the empirical message is clear: stronger classifiers alone are insufficient; coverage, prompt boundaries, feedback design, and termination policy are all first-order determinants of safety.41:T5ad,Read it in the order of threat model, evaluation stack, attack classes, and design lessons. Start with the introduction to anchor the distinction between accidental misuse and persistent adversarial agents. Then inspect Table 1 and Table 2 carefully; they define the attack categories, monitor stages, cwd-edit coverage, and strict variants that drive the rest of the paper.

Next, focus on the three evaluation modes in the approach section. The replay-versus-rollout distinction is especially important. Replay is cheap and useful for optimization and regression testing; rollout is the on-policy estimate that better reflects real risk. If you work on training or safety evaluation, this layered methodology is directly reusable. After that, read the appendices on worked examples, attacker affordances, OPRO-style injection optimization, and the fuzzing agent. Those sections explain why the attacks are not one-off anecdotes.

If time is limited, prioritize: the monitor pipeline description, representative bypass transcripts, benign replay false-positive results, and the comparison of monitor variants under malign replay and rollout. Two questions are worth digging into. First, how much does block-reason feedback help the attacker learn the decision boundary? Second, which failures are due to model limitations versus system-integration mistakes? The former matters for training-signal design; the latter matters for deployment architecture.42:T88b,方法核心是 SIFT：Recursive Self-Improvement via Fast Tree-search。整体仍然保留“上层 self-improving model 读当前 agent 代码和历史诊断信息，写出 patch，形成 child harness，再继续搜索”的递归框架，但把搜索和昂贵评估解耦。

第一步是引入 LLM judge 做成对比较。新生成的候选 harness 不再立刻依赖完整 benchmark 分数来决定是否继续扩展，而是先与少量已有强节点做 pairwise comparison。judge 看的不是最终 benchmark 结果，而是候选修改相对 incumbent harness 的质量，输出胜负关系。

第二步是用 regularized Bradley-Terry 模型把这些局部胜负关系聚合成全局 strength score。这样每个节点即使没有完整 benchmark 分数，也能得到一个可比较的排序信号。这里的关键不是 judge 单次打分是否绝对准确，而是通过 pairwise preference 和 BT 聚合，得到更稳定的相对强弱估计。

第三步是 fully disaggregated tree search。扩展和评估并行进行：一边继续从 archive 里选 parent 生成新 patch，一边把高潜力节点放入 evaluation priority queue 做更昂贵的真实 benchmark 验证。父节点采样和待评估节点排序都由一个混合指标控制，综合 judge rank、subset accuracy rank 和 visit-count exploration term。也就是说，它不是完全相信 judge，也不是完全相信小样本 benchmark，而是把两种不完美信号和探索项混合起来。

第四步是 speculative exploration。因为 judge 分数可以很快得到，系统不必等待慢速 evaluation 完成后再决定下一步扩展，而是先根据中间信号继续向前搜索。这个设计比传统“评完一个再扩一个”的串行循环更接近异步 best-first search。

和 DGM、SICA 这类已有方法相比，区别不在自修改对象，而在搜索控制层：SIFT 把 expensive evaluation 从主循环里拿出来，降级为稀疏但高价值的校准信号；把便宜 judge 提升为主导搜索流向的中间反馈。这个取舍的前提是：judge 虽然有偏，但排序能力足够好，能在大多数时候把明显差的 patch 挡在外面。43:T5ff,The method, SIFT, keeps the standard recursive self-improvement setup: a higher-level model inspects the current agent code and diagnostics, proposes a patch, and creates a child harness that may perform better on downstream coding tasks. The key change is that expansion and expensive evaluation are decoupled.

New candidates are first assessed through pairwise LLM-as-a-judge comparisons against a small set of strong existing nodes. Instead of asking the judge for an absolute score, SIFT collects win-loss outcomes between candidate harnesses. These pairwise outcomes are then aggregated with a regularized Bradley-Terry model, producing a global strength estimate for each node in the archive.

Search decisions are based on a mixture of three signals: judge-based rank, subset-benchmark accuracy rank, and a visit-count penalty that encourages exploration. Parent nodes for further expansion are sampled from the full archive using this combined score. In parallel, a priority queue determines which nodes should receive expensive benchmark evaluation next.

This creates a fully disaggregated pipeline. Expansion can continue using the fast judge-derived signal without waiting for slow benchmark runs to finish. Expensive evaluation becomes a sparse calibration mechanism rather than the gatekeeper for every search step. Compared with earlier self-improving agents such as SICA or DGM, the novelty is less about what gets modified and more about how the search tree is controlled, ranked, and scheduled under limited compute.44:T502,The headline results are on the Polyglot benchmark. SIFT reaches 35% with o3-mini as the base coding model and 32% with Qwen3-30B, while keeping runtime relatively low. The paper reports under 250 CPU hours and about 7 hours of wall-clock time for Qwen3, and under 50 CPU hours with about 5 hours wall-clock time for o3-mini.

The empirical claim is mainly about search efficiency rather than raw benchmark leadership. The method is intended to find useful self-modifications faster by avoiding full downstream evaluation for every patch. Another notable result is transferability: harnesses discovered with one coding model appear to transfer reasonably well to another, suggesting that the search is improving the orchestration layer rather than overfitting to a single backbone.

The most important experimental checks are likely the following. First, whether pairwise judge scores aggregated through Bradley-Terry correlate well enough with true benchmark outcomes to support ranking. Second, ablations on the mixed search policy: judge-only, accuracy-only, and removing the exploration term. Third, how much of the gain comes from better ranking versus asynchronous pipeline parallelism. Those details matter more than the top-line percentages if one wants to reuse the method.45:T445,The paper starts from the setting where the task horizon H is much larger than the context window C, process lifetime P, human attention interval A, and component failure interval F. It derives seven bottlenecks: Autonomy, Horizon, State, Resilience, Delegation, Correctness, and Cost. The proposed architecture has four abstractions. A level is a time-scale layer that keeps a bounded file summarizing the layer below and sends sparse directives downward. A tick is one autonomous action cycle: read state, reason, act, and write state back. A protocol is a named file or channel with specified writer, reader, cadence, and check. A tier is an intelligence level, including judgment models, labor models, and deterministic processes. Cascaded intelligence routes work upward when the cost of being wrong justifies a stronger model or human attention. Downward channels carry steering, while upward channels carry compressed state, back-pressure, escalation lines, and kill verdicts. The design avoids relying solely on longer context; it makes external state explicit, bounded, and auditable.15:["$","main",null,{"className":"flex-1 overflow-y-auto","children":["$","div",null,{"className":"mx-auto max-w-4xl px-4 py-8 sm:px-6 xl:max-w-6xl","children":["$","$L16",null,{"data":{"date":"2026-09-21","generated_at":"2026-09-21T00:52:10.204835+00:00","stats":{"total":123,"starred":4,"must_read":14,"skim":58,"glance":51,"by_category":{"pretraining_data":4,"industry":1,"llm_pretrain":3,"scaling":3,"architecture":13,"efficient":14,"eval":15,"multimodal":8,"multimodal_gen":4,"post_train":17,"mechanism":19,"coding_agent":7,"agent":15},"trending_repos":9,"trending_hf":10,"fetch_status":{"arxiv":"ok","huggingface":"empty","blog":"ok","twitter":"empty","github_trending":"ok","hf_trending":"ok"},"fetch_errors":{},"last_fetch_attempt":1789955160.971038},"categories":[{"id":"pretraining_data","emoji":"📚","name":"预训练数据","description":"以预训练数据为核心贡献的工作：数据采集/清洗/去重、数据配比(data mixture)、数据筛选/质量评估、语料构建、数据课程(data curriculum)、数据重复(repetition)、合成数据用于预训练、Tokenizer/词表设计、成员推断/数据检测。判据是『论文核心是数据本身』，而非训练 recipe/优化器(→ llm_pretrain)或评测资产(→ eval)","papers":[{"id":"2609.19291","title":"Why Pretraining Fails to Share Cross-Lingual Knowledge","abstract":"Large Language Models (LLMs) have made remarkable progress in the processing and modeling of many languages. w","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"must_read","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-09-16T18:02:29Z","category_id":"pretraining_data","cover_url":"/covers/2609_19291.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.CL","cs.AI","cs.LG"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇论文要回答一个很具体的问题：多语言模型在预训练阶段是否已经把不同语言中的知识分开存储，导致某个事实只在一种语言中见过时，不能有效迁移到另一种语言。难点在于真实预训练语料里很难追踪一个事实在各语言中的曝光次数，自然语言之间又同时差异于词表、脚本、语法和频率分布，直接比较很容易混入无关因素。","importance":"贡献主要在两个层面。第一，它把 cross-lingual knowledge compartmentalization 的来源定位到预训练阶段，而不是只把问题归因于指令微调或偏好对齐后的行为偏差。第二，它给 tokenizer 和词表设计提供了更直接的机制证据：语言之间的 token space 是否相交，会显著影响知识能否共享。对多语言基座训练来说，这比单纯讨论语料比例或语言覆盖更靠近训练机制。论文提出的 CL_eq 指标也有价值，因为它用“外语曝光的收益 / 本语曝光的收益”来度量迁移，避免把目标语言能力提升误读成跨语言知识迁移提升。","technical_approach":"方法核心是可控预训练。作者构造 Fictional Knowledge Dataset，包含 2,048 条关于合成实体和属性的虚构事实。每条事实被扩展成多语言自然文档，用于注入预训练；同时保留多选题用于评测。这样可以精确控制一个事实在语言 A 和语言 B 中分别出现多少次。模型训练后，作者用语言 A 的问题测试该事实，并用 OLS 回归把准确率分解为本语曝光贡献和跨语言曝光贡献。CL_eq_{B→A} 定义为跨语言曝光系数与本语曝光系数的比值，100% 表示 B 语言中的一次曝光和 A 语言中的一次曝光同样有效，0% 表示没有迁移。为了排除自然语言差异，论文还设计了 cloned language copies：语言结构相同，只改变 token 空间。这个设计把问题收窄到 token space 是否重叠，而不是脚本、语序或语义分布。主要机制结论是，词表中相互隔离的 token 区域会阻碍知识共享，并且这种阻碍在 360M 和 7B 规模上都存在。基于这个结论，作者提出 word-wise translation intervention，用逐词翻译的方式把词表不相交语言更有效地映射到共享 token 空间，从而提高跨语言知识泛化。","experiments":"实验从零训练双语模型，并在预训练过程中注入虚构事实。模型规模包括 360M 参数的 SmolLM2 风格模型，以及 7B 参数的 Llama-3 改造模型；两者使用 BPE tokenizer，词表规模为 65,536。作者先验证 FKD 的可学习性：0 注入时多选题接近随机水平；随着事实 paraphrase 注入次数增加，准确率呈近似对数式增长，说明数据集可以控制知识曝光并被模型吸收。主实验通过不同语言对、不同 tokenization 条件、自然双语和 cloned languages 测量 CL_eq。结果显示，知识 compartmentalization 在预训练中已经出现；当两种语言落在不相交 token space 时，跨语言知识曝光的价值显著低于本语曝光。附录还用高频人名构造的 fictive-persona dataset 复现实验，以排除“合成实体低频 token”导致的特例。word-wise translation intervention 同时改善 CL_eq 和一般语言建模 perplexity，说明它不是只针对 FKD 评测做过拟合，而是改善了模型处理词表隔离语言的训练信号。","reading_guide":"建议精读 §2 的 FKD 与 CL_eq 定义，这是论文最重要的测量工具。重点看 OLS 系数如何对应 native exposure 和 foreign exposure，以及为什么用比例而不是绝对准确率。§4 是判断 compartmentalization 是否出现在预训练阶段的关键。§5 intr","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"must_read","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-09-16T17:35:09Z","category_id":"llm_pretrain","cover_url":"/covers/2609_19242.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.LG"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇论文解决 corrupted copy x_t^(b) 做去噪，整体 loss 是各 block loss L_b 的和。关键观察是：这些目标 block 的 loss 在给定 clean 序列后可以分开计算。常规实现会构造 clean copy 与 corrupted copy 的长度 2L 表示，再用 block-causal mask 统一跑 attention；配合 CP 时，clean K/V 和 corrupted K/V 都被位置切分并跨 rank 通信。问题在于 clean prefix 会被多个 later blocks 复用，适合共享；corrupted K/V 只被本 block query 使用，不应跨卡通信。论文先提出 BP：把每个完整 corrupted-block computation 分配给一个 rank，不同 rank 并行处理不同目标 block，本 block 的 corrupted Q/K/V、activation、gradient 都留在本地，从而去掉 block-specific cross-rank communication。但 BP 会复制越来越长、且高度重叠的 clean prefix。为了解决这个问题，论文提出 CSBP：在同一组 rank 上同时做 block parallelism 和 clean-context This is clean and scalable, but it explores and allocates reward inefficiently: a trajectory may contain many causal decisions, recovery attempts, and environment-randomness events, yet every token or action inherits one trajectory-level advantage. We study tree-based rollout construction as a compute-allocation ","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-09-17T10:08:48Z","category_id":"scaling","cover_url":"/covers/2609_20004.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.LG","cs.AI"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇工作处理的是基于结果奖励的 on-policy RL 在长轨迹、带环境随机性的语言模型训练里，梯度信用分配过于粗糙的问题。GRPO 这类方法把整条采样轨迹压成一个终局标量奖励，再把同一个 advantage 回传给路径上的所有 token 或动作。这样会同时带来三类误差：一是恢复行为被误罚或误奖，正确中间步骤会被失败终局一并拖累；二是环境随机性和策略选择混在一起，单次 rollout 无法区分“动作不好”还是“后续运气差”；三是增加独立 rollout 数量时，算力常浪费在低价值分歧上，比如表述差异、格式差异或无关噪声。作者把 tree rollout 明确重述为一个梯度估计的算力分配问题：分支不该放在模型最不确定的地方，而该放在额外一个分支最能降低 policy-gradient 估计误差、且单位计算收益最高的地方。","importance":"对预训练/后训练接口的意义在于，它给出了一个比“高熵就分叉”更扎实的树搜索分配原则，尤其适合可验证奖励、长链推理、多轮 agent 环境这类现在越来越常见的训练场景。它的贡献不只是提出一个启发式算法，而是先给出方差分解，再据此推导分支和 suffix 重采样的最优分配律。这个角度很重要，因为很多 LLM RL 工作已经意识到 trajectory-level reward 太粗，但常见替代方案要么依赖 process reward model，要么靠经验规则选分叉点。这里的价值在于：不引入额外 critic 或 PRM，也能把“哪里值得多采样”写成和梯度 MSE 直接相关的量。对做 reasoning RL、tool-use RL、multi-turn agent RL 的人，这比单纯提升 benchmark 分数更有方法论价值。","technical_approach":"$1b","experiments":"实验覆盖四类设置，目的是验证理论而不只是展示单一任务收益。第一类是 cloned-state continuous control，用来在可控环境里检验分支位置和 suffix 分配是否符合理论推导。第二类是 frozen LLM gradient calibration，作者在冻结语言模型上比较不同树构造策略对梯度估计误差的影响，这一部分很关键，因为它直接对应论文声称的优化目标：不是只看最终 reward，而是看谁更接近真实梯度。第三类是 single-turn LLM math，验证在可验证奖励的推理任务上，EPIG-Tree 是否比独立 rollout 或熵驱动分叉更高效。第四类是 online multi-turn Wordle，测试在多步、带恢复机会、带环境随机性的 setting 下，树分配是否更能改善学习信号。\n\n从全文给出的结论看，EPIG-Tree 的优势主要体现在两种场景：一是存在明显 continuation noise，需要从同一前缀做 counterfactual suffix 比较；二是长轨迹里只有少数决策真正影响终局，平均扩展会浪费算力。作者也明确给出边界条件：如果环境几乎确定、轨迹很短、或者高熵位置本身就和高价值分歧高度重合，那么更便宜的 entropy baseline 会接近甚至追平 EPIG-Tree。这一点是可信的，因为它说明方法收益来自更精细的算力分配，而不是无条件优于所有树方法。摘要和正文片段没有给出完整数表，因此这里不虚构具体百分点；但从实验组织看，作者重点报告的是梯度校准误差、单位计算预算下的任务回报、以及在不同 branching budget 下的收益曲线，而不是单一终局指标。","reading_guide":"如果只花一小时，优先看 Introduction、方差分解命题、EPIG 定义与算法部分、以及 frozen LLM gradient calibration 和 Wordle 两组实验。真正的创新点不在“树 rollout”四个字，而在于作者把树看成梯度实验装置，并据此区分了 decision uncertainty 和 continuation uncertainty。读的时候建议盯住三个问题：第一，命题 1 的分解里哪些量是可估的，哪些量只能近似；第二，EPIG 在实现时如何避免自己变成一个比训练还贵的估计器；第三，为什么 entropy 适合做 proposal，但不适合做最终 allocation criterion。\n\n值得深挖的地方有两个。一个是 occupancy 加权的作用：在 LLM 里，高频前缀和低频前缀的梯度价值不同，这和主动学习里的信息增益并不完全一样。另一个是 suffix allocation law，它提示未来可以把 tree RL 和 adaptive compute、speculative branching、甚至 verifier-guided rollout 结合起来。若要判断这篇方法能否落地到大规模 reasoning RL，重点看算法复杂度、并行实现方式，以及在在线训练中是否需要额外状态克隆或环境可回溯能力。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper addresses a structural weakness of outcome-based on-policy RL for language models: trajectory-level rewards are too coarse for long-horizon, stochastic, and recovery-heavy settings. Methods such as GRPO assign one terminal scalar advantage to every token or action on a sampled trajectory. This creates three coupled errors: correct intermediate actions can be penalized when the final outcome fails, bad actions can be reinforced when later steps recover, and environment randomness is confounded with policy quality. Simply increasing the number of independent rollouts is also compute-inefficient, because many samples differ only in low-value surface variations. The paper reframes tree rollouts as a compute allocation problem for policy-gradient estimation: branching should happen where an extra branch reduces gradient uncertainty the most per unit cost, not merely where the policy has high entropy.","importance":"This matters for the pretrain–posttrain interface because RL for reasoning, tool use, and multi-turn agents is increasingly limited by credit assignment quality rather than by raw reward availability. The contribution is not just another tree-search heuristic. The paper derives a variance decomposition for local policy-gradient estimation and uses it to obtain principled allocation rules for branching and suffix resampling. That is a stronger foundation than entropy-based branching or process-reward-model-heavy pipelines. For researchers working on verifiable-reward RL, long-chain reasoning, or agent training, the main value is methodological: it gives a direct objective for where extra rollout compute should go if the goal is better gradient estimates.","technical_approach":"$1c","experiments":"$1d","reading_guide":"$1e","authors_note":""},"recommendation":{"zh":"这篇值得看，因为它把 RL rollout branching 从“搜索技巧”拉回到“梯度方差/算力分配”问题；先看方差分解和 allocation law，若实验干净应尽快复现。","en":"Worth reading because it reframes rollout branching from a search trick into a gradient-variance / compute-allocation problem; start with the variance decomposition and allocation laws, and reproduce if the setup is clean."},"metadata":{"institutions":[],"authors":["Nikita Khomich","Leopold Hermansson","Ido Hakimi"],"notable_authors":[],"tags":["rl-for-llm","compute-allocation","policy-gradient","variance-reduction","grpo"],"links":{"arxiv":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20004","pdf":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.20004","github":null,"project":null,"source_url":null,"article":null}}},{"id":"2609.20218","title":"Is It Still Worth Training a Classical Model in the Era of LLMs? Many self-consistency and multi-sample reasoning studies report N but omit calls per query, candidates per call, and measured energy. That makes systems comparisons hard to reproduce. The practical lesson is that test-time compute scaling should be specified as an execution schedule, not just as a sample count.","technical_approach":"The authors formalize a generation schedule as S=(b1,...,bC), where C is the number of sequential generation calls and bc is the number of candidates generated in call c. All schedules use the same prompts, decoding settings, answer extraction, and plurality voting. There is no verifier, reward model, or token-level scoring. They track logical input and output token volumes to ensure that measured systems differences are not mainly caused by different response lengths. For accuracy scaling, they generate eight candidates and compute paired accuracies using the first N candidates for N in {1,2,3,4,8}. sorting、ordered_sampling、以及 loss 归一化。这里决定了它为什么还能在 causal mask 下做类似 diffusion 的学习。接着看 Figure 1 和 slot_step_borders 相关段落，理解 slot curriculum 如何改变训练难度，以及为什么作者提醒“大 slot 会抬高 loss”。如果时间有限，实验部分优先看三类内容：一是与纯
